--[p313]-- Then, the leveling sensor detects the reflected light from the wafer.
Inclination shift from the image plane on the wafer surface is detected as position shift of the reflected light at the light-receiving section.
The leveling sensor is adjusted so that the wafer inclination when the reflected light was detected at the detector center can become the best level.
In the leveling process, wafer surface is adjusted by moving up and down three drive points (two points in NSR-i11 series) of Z motor assembly located under the wafer table.

Energy amount = (Unit energy amount) x (Exposure Time (c)/(p)),

===== [p159-159] 4.2 CONDITIONS FOR EXECUTING BASELINE CHECK > Measuring the Shift in the X Direction (4块) =====
--[p159]-- To measure the shift in the X direction of a shot array, the X mark (in at least one of the two s-shots) is measured.
To determine the measurement position for the marks, the correction factors derived in step 2 are added to the measurement target position determined in advance in prealignment assist.
In the measurement, as with the Y mark, the shift, ∆Xi, of the mark from its measurement target position is measured.
--[p159]-- $$ ( \Delta \mathsf { X } \mathsf { i } , \Delta \mathsf { Y } \mathsf { i } ) = ( \mathsf { X } \mathsf { i } , \mathsf { Y } \mathsf { i } ) - \mathsf { G } _ { \mathsf { p r e } } \circ \mathsf { F } _ { \mathsf { d e s i g n } \to \mathsf { p r e } _ { - } \mathsf { a s s i s t } } \left( \mathsf { X } \mathsf { i } , \mathsf { y } \mathsf { i } \right) \qquad \mathsf { i } = 3 , 4 $$
where (Xi, Yi) : X mark detected position (wafer stage coordinate system),
--[p159]-- $( \mathsf { x i } , \mathsf { y i } )$ : X mark design position (wafer coordinate system),
Gpre : adjustment function of wafer coordinate system (axis) calculated from Wafer Pre-alignment Adjustment (m),
Fdesign→pre\_assist : correction function of wafer coordinate derived in the prealignment assist (The correction factors derived in (2) are also added.),
∆Xi : measurement result of X direction shift.
The user can choose whether to measure the shift in either of the s-shots or in both of them.
--[p159]-- The user can choose whether to measure the shift in either of the s-shots or in both of them.
From the above results, the following correction factors are calculated:
• Wafer offset X factor
• Wafer scaling factor (NOTE)

===== [p148-148] 4.2 CONDITIONS FOR EXECUTING BASELINE CHECK > Wafer Coordinates Adjustment (m) (2块) =====
--[p148]-- An adjustment function of Wafer Coordinates Adjustment (m) is the base function to correct the wafer coordinate system, and it is developed to be more accurate correction function gradually by executing wafer alignment (search alignment and EGA).
--[p148]-- Specifies the adjustment value for converting the shot coordinates arrayed on the wafer coordinate system.
Shot array set in the process program is designed based on the wafer coordinate system which is determined by the wafer loading position and Wafer Pre-alignment Adjustment (m).

1. Alignment marks must be printed in the shots exposed on the first layer. New alignment marks may be reprinted on an arbitrary subsequent layer.

--[p154]-- This function is then combined with the correction function, Fdesign→ pre\_assist, that was calculated in pre-alignment assist. The result of this composition can be used to move the projected image closer to shots on the previous layer.
In each shot exposed on the previous layer, search marks to be measured in search alignment have been exposed in addition to product patterns (NOTE 1) . In search alignment, a Y mark and an X mark are measured in each of the two sample shots (sshots) (NOTE 2) .
--[p154]-- Wafers on which pre-alignment assist has been completed next undergo search alignment.
In search alignment, only two shots are selected and measured as sample shots from all the shots exposed on the wafer. From the results of this measurement, error factors (wafer factors) are computed, and these error factors are used to derive a correction function, Fsrc, which is used to estimate the rough position of all of the shots.
--[p154]-- From the results of this measurement, the following error factors (NOTE 3) as well as the function, Fsrc, that is created from them, are computed.
• Wafer offset factor Shift factor in the X and Y directions used to correctly overlay the center of the shot array onto the center of the shot array of the previous layer.
• Wafer rotation factor A rotation factor used to correctly overlay the orientation of the shot array onto that of the shot array on the previous layer.
--[p155]-- For products that require a lesser degree of overlay accuracy, exposure can be performed right after search alignment; EGA can be skipped. In that case, this correction function is used to compute the positions of the exposure shots.

===== [p198-198] 5.7 ERROR PROCESSING IN WAFER SEARCH ALIGNMENT > ◆ Auto. g-EGA or Auto. t-EGA (1块) =====

===== [p119-119] 2.2.1 Reticle Stage Coordinate System (3块) =====
--[p119]-- The result is converted to the reticle stage coordinate by using the function stored in NSR. In this conversion function, the position of the interferometer corner mirror in the condition that the stage locates at the origin is used.
The stage position is indicated by the position of the center of the reticle holder in the reticle stage coordinate system.
--[p119]-- The reticle stage coordinate system is an orthogonal coordinate system that indicates the position of the reticle stage. Its origin is directly above the center of the reduction projection lens. Its coordinate system, however, is rotated by 180 o with respect to the wafer stage coordinate system.
Position of the reticle stage is measured by the reticle stage interferometers (laser interferometers, one on the X axis and two on the Y axis).
--[p119]-- For example, when the center of the reticle holder is immediately above the center of the reduction projection lens, the stage is at the origin (0,0) of the coordinate system.

===== [p108-108] (3) ISS (Image Slit Sensor) (3块) =====
--[p108]-- The ISS is used to measure the position of the reticle alignment mark or the ISS mark on the reticle. In the NSR of i11 and i12 series, ISS is used in the simultaneous baseline check with LSA sensors (wafer alignment sensors of scanning system) and the reticle interferometer calibration. However, NSR-4425i cannot be equipped with ISS.
--[p108]-- The following graph indicates the relationship between the wafer stage position and brightness at the measurement.
In the reticle alignment mark measurement using ISS, the part where brightness is lowest in this graph is regarded as the time when the fiducial mark passed across a reticle alignment mark. Then, with the wafer stage position, the reticle alignment mark position on the wafer stage coordinate system can be calculated.
--[p108]-- However, NSR-4425i cannot be equipped with ISS.
In the measurement, the wafer stage is scanned on the condition that the same light as the exposure light is being emitted through the slit of fiducial plate (fiducial mark for ISS) located under the reduction projection lens and the slit image enlarged through the projection lens is being focused onto the reticle surface.
By this operation, the slit image passes across reticle alignment marks, and then light and dark caused by that slit image is detected at the ISS light-receiving section.

===== [p233-233] 5.7 ERROR PROCESSING IN WAFER SEARCH ALIGNMENT > [4] EGA Reject Limit (p) (1块) =====
--[p233]-- Specifies the tolerance used to judge whether an EGA measurement result value is aberrant in EGA.
Each sensor has its own individual parameter: LSA-EGA Reject Limit (p), FIA-EGA Reject Limit (p), and LIA-EGA Reject Limit (p).

===== [p301-301] [1] Focus Offset Adjustment (m) (1块) =====
--[p301]-- Specifies a displacement (that is unique to the machine) from the focus position detected with the AF sensor. When the NSR is installed, this value should be determined by performing an evaluation exposure using a test reticle.
Nikon recommends that an evaluation exposure be performed once a week to maintain the proper focus. The results of this periodic maintenance operation should be used to update the value of Focus Offset Adjustment (m).

===== [p285-285] [3] Blind Center Shift (p) (1块) =====
--[p285]-- Specifies the amount to shift the shot element (on the shot) defined by Reticle Blind (p). The values are specified for X and Y directions in the wafer shot coordinate system.
The previous figure shows an example in which blind ID “1” is specified and also a composite shot is created for the ID type.
The following figures give an example of exposure in which blind ID “\*” is specified in addition to the abovementioned conditions (blind ID “1” and a composite shot setting).

===== [p229-229] 5.7 ERROR PROCESSING IN WAFER SEARCH ALIGNMENT > [2] EGA Result Logging Switch (s) (1块) =====
--[p229]-- Standard setting: “No EGA Result Logging”
This parameter specifies whether to store EGA results to a file and retain them.
◆ No EGA Result Logging The results are not stored.
◆ EGA Result Logging The results are stored.

===== [p180-180] 4.2 CONDITIONS FOR EXECUTING BASELINE CHECK > (3) Around Search (Wafer Search Alignment) (3块) =====
--[p180]-- When a measurement error occurs in wafer search alignment, the automatic remeasurement and automatic search range extension functions are tried as automatic recovery operations. If these processes fail to detect the mark, shots adjacent to the target s-shots (around shots) are measured as s-shots instead.
Execution of the around search (wafer search alignment) is determined by the setting of Around Search Limit (Search) (s).
--[p180]-- Around Search Limit (Search) (s) specifies the maximum number of around shots on which the around search is executed. There are eight around shots in all. The measurement order of the around shots is automatically determined. If the search mark cannot be measured after retrying measurement the number of times specified by Around Search Limit (Search) (s), the automatic reject starts.
Specifies the maximum times that around search may be repeated in wafer search alignment.
--[p180]-- Specifies the maximum times that around search may be repeated in wafer search alignment.
◆ 0 No around search is performed.
◆ 1 to 8

===== [p310-310] 6.1.5 Alternative Methods When Normal Focusing Is Unfeasible (Shift Focusing) \~ Focusing for Shots on the Wafer Edge \~ (2块) =====
--[p310]-- When normal focusing is impossible for a shot on the edge of a wafer, performing shift focusing is examined.
When normal focusing is deemed impossible, the measurement points on the wafer are shifted toward the inside of the wafer, and then measurement and focusing are performed. This is called the shift focusing.
Shift focusing is performed by specifying “Shift Focus” for Shift Focusing Mode (p) when normal focusing is unfeasible (NOTE) .
--[p310]-- When the shot is the first one to be exposed on the wafer and normal focusing is impossible, shift focusing will be performed even if Shift Focusing Mode (p) is set to “No Shift Focus”.
There are two methods of shifting in the shift focusing: intrashot shift focusing and shot shift focusing. Specify the desired method using the Shift Focusing Position (s).

===== [p314-314] • Best level when leveling is performed (2块) =====
--[p314]-- Based on the results of measurements using the leveling sensor, the shot surface is adjusted to the image plane of the reduction projection lens so that the shot face will be parallel to the image plane. The adjusted shot surface is called the leveling adjustment plane. The best level is the level derived using the below formula which is based on the leveling adjustment plane. The level of the shot surface is eventually adjusted to this best level.
Best level = (Leveling adjustment plane)

===== [p61-62] (1) Reticle ID Name Extraction and Comparison with Reticle Name (3块) =====
--[p61]-- Reticle ID check examines whether the reticle ID name in the barcode which has been read by the reticle barcode reader matches Reticle Name (c)/(p) specified as a parameter. The reticle ID name character string extracted from the barcode is defined by Matching Field of Reticle Name (s).
Reticle Name (c)/(p) is used for comparison with the reticle ID name.
Specifies the start and end positions of the part extracted as the reticle ID name from the barcode which has been read (NOTE) .
--[p62]-- ◆ 1 to 64 alphanumeric characters, special characters, and space
Special characters “\*” and “%” are recognized as wildcards when bringing out the reticle from the reticle library. When a character specified by Reticle Name Delimiter (s) is used, all characters after that character are treated as comments.
--[p61]-- ◆ 1 to 44 23 (Start)
◆ 1 to 44 28 (End)
NOTE
According to the barcode standard, the maximum barcode character number differs as follows:
In case of the NIKON barcode (NIKON original barcode standard): 44
• In case of the Code 39 :28
The number of the reticle ID name characters are extracted from the beginning of the Reticle Name (c)/(p) characters, and the character string is compared with the reticle ID name extracted from the barcode.

===== [p178-178] 4.2 CONDITIONS FOR EXECUTING BASELINE CHECK > (1) Automatic Remeasurement (1块) =====

Standard setting: “8”
Specifies the maximum times that measurement can be repeated when an error occurs in measurement.
In the case of not succeeding in the measurement after the measurement has been performed again by the number of times set in Search Alignment Error Try Count (s), perform the automatic extension of the search range.

===== [p259-260] 5.7 ERROR PROCESSING IN WAFER SEARCH ALIGNMENT > (1) Correcting the Shot Rotation in the First Layer (3块) =====
--[p259]-- In the first exposure, the shots are exposed according to a layout that conforms exactly to the design specification. Thus, there is no need to deliberately correct shot orientation.
If the reticle on the reticle stage, however, is not set correctly, each shot is rotated and eventually a shot array is exposed as stairs. In that case, some method must be employed of measuring and correcting the rotation of each shot so that the 2nd and subsequent layers will be correctly overlaid.
--[p260]-- The amount of shot rotation correction on the wafer stage coordinate system is calculated according to the following formula (NOTE 1) :
Amount of shot rotation correctio $1 = R _ { \mathrm { p r e 2 } } + \mathsf { R w } + \mathsf { R s } + \mathsf { R e } ,$
where Rpre : Wafer Pre-alignment Adjustment (m),
RW : wafer rotation factor (normally “0”),
RS : shot rotation factor (normally “0”),
RR : reticle rotation factor (NOTE 1) .
--[p259]-- To prevent such a nuisance, the reticle alignment marks are measured in the reticle alignment check, and the reticle stage is rotated by an amount corresponding to the reticle rotation factor derived from this measurement.
Wafer coordinate system and shot position before correction Wafer coordinate system and shot position after stepping is corrected using reticle rotation factor derived in reticle alignment check

===== [p308-308] • Algorithm 0 (1块) =====
--[p308]-- In this algorithm, all the five sensors must be within focusing feasible area. If any one of them is out of focusing feasible area, multipoint focusing is not performed at the relevant shot.

===== [p107-107] (2) VRA Sensor (Video Reticle Alignment Sensor) (3块) =====
--[p107]-- Then, with this shift amount and the wafer stage position at the measurement, the reticle alignment mark position on the wafer stage coordinate system can be calculated.
--[p107]-- The VRA sensors are used to measure the position of the reticle alignment mark. These sensors are used in the reticle alignment check. In NSR-4425i for which modification to improve the throughput has been executed, VRA is used also in the reticle interferometer calibration.
--[p107]-- In the measurement, an image of the fiducial mark for VRA on the fiducial plate, which is focused onto the reticle surface through the projection lens, and the reticle alignment mark are simultaneously acquired by the reticle alignment microscope.
Images are captured into CCD cameras, image processing is executed, and then the amount of alignment mark position shift on the basis of the fiducial mark is obtained.

===== [p164-165] 4.2 CONDITIONS FOR EXECUTING BASELINE CHECK > (2) Position and Configuration of Search Marks in s-shots (4块) =====
--[p164]-- Thus, a single mark is susceptible to misrecognition of its identification.
With a double mark or a triple mark, the mark pitch is used as a judgment standard in addition to the prohibited zone width, making these marks harder misrecognized. This is especially true of triple marks. For example, a triple mark with three lines configured in unequal pitches is extremely resistant to misdetection. Settings related to the position and configuration of a mark are made by search mark data in the process program.
--[p164]-- A mark’s configuration is defined by such information as the mark pitch, prohibited zone width, and mark width.
When the mark is a single-line type, the only requirement is that another signal does not exist within its left and right prohibited zones. This is the only standard on which the NSR judges whether a signal is a real mark signal. Thus, a single mark is susceptible to misrecognition of its identification.
--[p165]-- Specifies the position of the search mark within the shot individually for the X and the Y marks. The mark center in the table below is specified as the mark position. The mark center differs depending on the mark type (single, double, or triple).
Marks in the table are images of the marks on a wafer. On the ITV screen, these images differ depending on marks.
5.4 MARKS MEASURED IN WAFER SEARCH ALIGNMENT
--[p164]-- The position of the search marks (X mark and Y mark) in each shot is specified in a coordinate system whose origin is the shot center (in the shot coordinate system). The mark position is specified with the coordinate of the mark center which is determined individually for each mark type. There are three types of marks: single-line marks (single marks), double-line marks (double marks), and triple-line marks (triple marks).

===== [p132-132] 4.1 FUNCTION AND PROCESSING SEQUENCE OF THE BASELINE CHECK (3块) =====
--[p132]-- However the actual baseline changes over time, so it is occasionally measured.
This measurement is called “baseline check.” In this processing the fiducial mark is measured by the sensor to be calibrated and correction value (baseline offset) for the constant “baseline” retained in the NSR is calculated.
Wafer alignment sensors to be used in the second exposure must undergo baseline check in advance. To avoid the changes in the baseline over time, the NSR performs baseline check when the reticle for the second layer undergoes alignment (immediately before exposure).
--[p132]-- Baseline is the relative distance between the center of the projection lens and each wafer alignment sensor. This relationship must be obtained correctly in order to execute precise overlay exposures (NOTE) .
NOTE
Each alignment measurement result can be properly converted to a value based on the center of the reticle by calculating the correct baseline.
The NSR retains the baseline as a constant. However the actual baseline changes over time, so it is occasionally measured.
--[p132]-- When exposure on numbers of wafers with the same reticle is repeated, changes in the baseline are expected to occur. Therefore, the machine is set to perform baseline checks at certain intervals during exposure. This processing is called “interval baseline check.”

===== [p330-330] b. Focus Calibration on Wafer Exposure (2块) =====
--[p330]-- When the variation in the control amount of the lens controller exceeds the value specified by Threshold of Requesting Focus Calibration (s), the interval focus calibration is executed at the time of wafer exchange immediately after the exceeding of the value.
--[p330]-- Focus calibration is conducted in wafer exposure depending on variations in the control amount of the lens controller. This is referred to as the interval focus calibration (NOTE) .
The interval focus calibration is activated when Interval Focus Calibration (p) is set to “Interval Focus Calib.” or to “Refer to S.P.” and besides “Interval Focus Calib.” is specified for Focus Calibration Switch (at Reticle Alignment) (s).

===== [p223-223] 5.7 ERROR PROCESSING IN WAFER SEARCH ALIGNMENT > 6.5.4 Measurement by LIA Sensor (3块) =====
--[p223]-- Alignment mark position is calculated from the obtained phase contrast.
There are the following two types of signal processing modes depending on whether using ±1st-order diffracted light or using 0-order and 2nd-order diffracted lights.
• 1st-order light processing mode ±1st-order diffracted light is used.
• 2nd-order light processing mode 0-order and 2nd-order diffracted lights are used.
In the case where strong enough beat signal cannot be obtained in the 1st-order light processing mode, use the 2nd-order light processing mode.
--[p223]-- Correct detection of a mark via signal processing requires selection of the appropriate signal processing conditions depending on the exposed layer state.
In the measurement with the LIA sensor, after digitalizing both the wafer alignment signal and the reference signal, phase contrast between two signals is calculated. Alignment mark position is calculated from the obtained phase contrast.
--[p223]-- The signal processing mode when using the LIA sensor is specified by the process program, LIA Signal Processing Algorithm (p).
Standard setting: “30”
Specifies the algorithm to detect the mark center from the signal data obtained in LIA.
◆ 30 1st-order light processing mode
◆ 32 2nd-order light processing mode

===== [p60-60] (4) Saving the Results of the Reticle Particle Check (3块) =====
--[p60]-- The number of particles is counted by the number of grids (1 mm x 1 mm grids of the detailed map) where particles exist. Even if particles exist both on glass face and on patternpellicle face of a grid, it is counted as one particle.
• PD5 series If any one of the number of particles exceeds the specified upper limit of the number among the number of particles on each checked face, the content of the detailed reticle map is not stored.
--[p60]-- The condition to save the results of reticle particle check in a file can be specified using File Saving Mode (s) and Detailed Map Saving Limit (s).
Sequence Mode
Specifies the mode used to save the reticle particle check result to a file.
◆ Temporary The latest reticle particle check result is saved in a individual file for each slot of the reticle library.
◆ Permanent All particle check results are saved in a individual file.
Sequence Mode

◆ 0 to 32767 0 [particle]

===== [p288-288] 2.2 STEPPING SEQUENCE (1块) =====
--[p288]-- The sequence in which shots are exposed depends on the stepping route and the stepping group:

===== [p101-101] • Reticle alignment (1块) =====
--[p101]-- By using the alignment marks on the reticle as a reference, the reticle attitude (reticle center and rotation) is measured. It is composed of the following subprocesses:
\- Reticle search
\- Reticle fine alignment
\- Reticle alignment check

===== [p175-175] 4.2 CONDITIONS FOR EXECUTING BASELINE CHECK > (1) Focusing in Search Alignment (2块) =====
--[p175]-- When using the LSA sensor, performing focusing is required at the position of the search mark just before the measurement. However, when using the FIA sensor, focusing is not necessary.
When performing focusing, the AF sensor sets the Z position so that the AF sensor will focus on the surface of the resist on the wafer. Therefore, if there is a substantial difference between the mark height and the height of the resist surface, an adjustment value is necessary to remove defocus.
--[p175]-- This adjustment value is set in Focus Offset in Alignment (p).
The Z position in search alignment is determined by the following formula:
Z position in search mark measurement = Z + ∆Z,
where Z : Z position derived by the AF sensor + Focus Offset in Exposure (p),
∆Z : AF Offset in Alignment (p),
Standard setting: “0.0”
Specify an adjustment value that is used to bring the surface of the alignment mark up to the same height as the focal plane.

===== [p187-187] 5.7 ERROR PROCESSING IN WAFER SEARCH ALIGNMENT > 6.1.2 EGA Calculation Model (2块) =====
--[p187]-- The factors calculated in EGA depend on the number of shots measured, the number of measurement points in a shot, and the EGA calculation model that is employed. There are five different EGA calculation models to choose from. Typically MCSV selects the EGA calculation model automatically based on the number of measurement shots and the number of measurement points in each shot.
--[p187]-- Alternatively, the user can intentionally select the model to suit the state of the previous layer and the required overlay accuracy. Note that the two-shot EGA model is restricted to error handling and is not allowed to be specified by the user.

===== [p301-301] [2] Focus Calibration Offset Adjustment (m) (2块) =====
--[p301]-- Specifies a displacement (that is unique to the machine) from the focus position derived from the results of focus calibration using the TTL-FC2 sensor. When the NSR is installed, this value should be determined by performing an evaluation exposure using a test reticle.
Nikon recommends that an evaluation exposure be performed by resetting the value once a week to maintain the proper focus.
--[p301]-- The results of this periodic maintenance operation should be used to update the value of Focus Calibration Offset Adjustment (m).

===== [p49-49] • Reticle is set in the library in advance (3块) =====
--[p49]-- This is called the RLAT reference method. For additional information on the method of creating this list, see Chapter 9, “ASSIGN Command,” in “NSR/MCSV Command Explanation (Part II SETUP Group).”
Specify the direct specification method or the RLAT reference method by using Reticle Slot No. Assignment Mode (s).
Specifies the method to specify Reticle Slot No. (c) in the exposure processing commands.
◆ Direct Specifies Reticle Slot No. (c) with the direct specification method.
--[p49]-- When the product (product layer) that is exposed on a particular NSR is more-orless fixed, the reticle to be used can be placed in the reticle library in advance.
In the case “reticle is set in the library in advance,” it is convenient to create beforehand a list that tells which reticle name corresponds with which slot number, and then activate the reticle library assignment table (RLAT) function for automatically referring to the list as the slot specification can be omitted.
--[p49]-- ◆ Direct Specifies Reticle Slot No. (c) with the direct specification method.
The user directly specifies the slot number of the reticle to be processed.
◆ RLAT Specifies Reticle Slot No. (c) with the RLAT reference method. The RLAT is automatically referred to, and the slot number that corresponds to Reticle Name (c)/(p) of the reticle to be processed is displayed.

===== [p134-134] 4.2.2 Execution Timing (2块) =====
--[p134]-- When the processing is done by READY command up to reticle alignment and done by EXECUTE command for the wafer exposure, there can be some interval before the wafer exposure. In this case, the setting “Off” is effective. This setting makes it possible to derive a more highly reliable baseline.
Standard setting: “On”
◆ Off The baseline check is performed right before exposure of the first wafer of the lot.
◆ On The baseline check is performed at the time of reticle alignment.
--[p134]-- The timing of baseline check can be changed by the specification of the BCHK after Reticle Alignment (s).
When this parameter is set to “On”, the baseline check is executed at the time of reticle alignment.
When it is set to “Off”, the baseline check is executed immediately before the exposure for the first wafer of the lot.

===== [p239-239] 5.7 ERROR PROCESSING IN WAFER SEARCH ALIGNMENT > (1) Multimark Detection Error Reject (2块) =====
--[p239]-- The method of this function (multimark detection error reject) is specified using LSA Multiple Mark Error Mode (p).
If this function fails to operate successfully, an alignment mark detection error is generated, and the automatic remeasurement function starts.
--[p239]-- In multimark measurement using the LSA sensor, extraction processing for each mark is performed. Multimark detection error reject makes measurement valid by using the measurement results of the successfully measured marks. (This works even when mark extraction did not operate successfully for all the marks.) This enables the NSR to avoid generating a detection error for an alignment mark.

===== [p296-296] 5.1 CORRECTING SHOT MAGNIFICATION BY LENS MAGNIFICATION CONTROL (2块) =====
--[p296]-- In the second exposure, lens magnification control is performed to match the size of a shot to be exposed to that of the shots in the previous layers.
Lens magnification control provides the following two types of control, the two aspects regarding the shot size taken into account.
--[p296]-- On the NSR, a correction amount for lens magnification is sent to the lens controller in exposure in order to adjust the magnification of the reduction projection lens. This correction amount takes into account the reticle elasticity rate as well as the scaling factors computed in wafer alignment (wafer scaling and shot scaling). In the first exposure, lens magnification control ensures that the shots are the same size as the design specification.

===== [p320-320] 6.2.5 Alternative Method When Normal Leveling Is Unfeasible (Shift Leveling) (3块) =====
--[p320]-- \~ Leveling for Shots on the Wafer Edge \~
When normal leveling is impossible, shift leveling is examined.
In shift leveling, the measurement points on the wafer are shifted toward the interior of the wafer, and then leveling is performed.
Shift leveling is performed when Leveling Switch (c)/(s) specifies “Shift Leveling” (NOTE 1) .
NOTE
1. Shift leveling is also performed when Leveling Switch (c)/(s) specifies “Refer to P.P. (Leveling)” and besides Leveling Mode (p) specifies “Shift”.
--[p320]-- (Leveling)” and besides Leveling Mode (p) specifies “Shift”.
In shift leveling, an alternative shot which meets conditions for normal leveling is found near the shot to be exposed and leveling is performed on it. After leveling on the alternative shot, the wafer (stage) is moved back to its original position and exposure begins (NOTE 2) .
NOTE
2. When the shot is the first one to be exposed on the wafer and normal leveling is impossible, shift leveling will be performed even if Leveling Switch (c)/(s) is set to “Leveling”.
--[p320]-- The alternative shot is automatically selected based on the position of the shot to be exposed.
However, if a suitable alternative shot cannot be found, the shot is exposed with the inclination for the immediately prior shot.
If there is no alternative shot for the first shot to be exposed on the wafer, perform leveling at a point within leveling feasible area as near the leveling position as possible.

When multiple measurement points are measured in a shot, the measurement result is regarded as that of a hypothetical point and is analyzed.

===== [p187-187] 5.7 ERROR PROCESSING IN WAFER SEARCH ALIGNMENT > • 6-parameter model (2块) =====
--[p187]-- The 6- parameter model differs from the intra-shot averaging model in that it handles all of the measurement results separately, when multiple measurement points are measured in a shot.
To apply this model, measurement results must be obtained from at least three shots, in each of which measurement must be made on at least one measurement point. When multiple measurement points are measured in each shot, it is better to use the intra-shot averaging model.
--[p187]-- This model calculates only the wafer factors. Wafer factors comprise six types of correction factors described below, which is the reason for its name.
\- Wafer offset factor X, Y
\- Wafer scaling factor X, Y
\- Wafer rotation factor
\- Wafer orthogonality factor
This calculation model computes only the wafer factors based on all the measurement result, regardless of the number of intra-shot measurement points.

===== [p30-30] 4.1 RETICLE ALIGNMENT SYSTEM (1块) =====
--[p30]-- Three types of sensors with different detection methods are installed for the reticle alignment system.
• SRA
• VRA
• ISS (NOTE)
SRA and VRA are located in the optical system of the reticle alignment microscope, which is used for detecting marks on the reticle, and ISS is located in the illumination system.
ISS is not supported in NSR-4425i.

===== [p18-19] 2.1 ILLUMINATION SYSTEM (3块) =====
--[p18]-- The illumination system optically processes exposure light from the super-high pressure mercury lamp to put it in the optimum condition before directing it to the reduction projection lens. The illumination system is composed of the following components:
• a super-high pressure mercury lamp that provides the exposure light source,
an ellipsoidal mirror that condense rays of light from the light source toward a direction,
--[p18]-- • a super-high pressure mercury lamp that provides the exposure light source,
an interference filter that passes only i-line wavelength component of the exposure light,
a fly’s eye lens that provides uniform distribution of light (by coupling an input lens and a condenser lens),
• illumination apertures that increase resolution, and
• an exposure shutter that controls the exposure time.
The optional modified illumination system can be installed. If using this optional system, the dedicated lens matched to the diaphragm condition is automatically selected.
--[p19]-- The reticle blinds shield non-exposure area on the reticle from exposure light. The blinds are two L-shaped metal blades which are operated opposite to each other. They can create an exposure area of an arbitrary size (a minimum 500 µm projected image on the wafer).

===== [p150-150] 4.2 CONDITIONS FOR EXECUTING BASELINE CHECK > (1) Manual Assist for Y Marks (3块) =====
--[p150]-- The starting position for a manual assist is the search mark position estimated on the basis of Wafer Pre-alignment Adjustment (m) and Wafer Coordinates Adjustment (m).
If the Y mark is not in the center of the field, drive the wafer stage in manual mode until it comes to the center, and when the position of the stage is determined, press the [EXEC] button on the operation panel.
At this time, the pre-alignment assist results (∆X, ∆Y) are expressed in the following formula:
--[p150]-- (∆X, ∆Y) : pre-alignment assist results.
The above operation is repeated for the two Y marks.
--[p150]-- (∆X, ∆Y) = (X, Y) - Gpre Fdesign→adj (x, y),
where (X, Y) : assist end position (wafer stage coordinate system),
(x, y) : Y mark design position (wafer coordinate system),
Gpre : adjustment function of wafer coordinate system (axis) calculated from Wafer Pre-alignment Adjustment (m),
Fdesign→adj : adjustment function derived from Wafer Coordinates Adjustment (m),
Gpre Fdesign→adj : composition of Fdesign→adj and $\mathsf { G } _ { \mathsf { p r e } }$
(∆X, ∆Y) : pre-alignment assist results.

===== [p10-10] 1.1 OVERVIEWS OF EXPOSURE BY NSR (3块) =====
--[p10]-- The wafer stage is then moved to the next exposure site, and the exposure processing is performed.
Both these operations are repeated until the entire wafer is exposed, which is called “step and repeat system.”
Both the wafer and reticle are vacuum-fixed onto the surface of holders on their respective stages.
The positions of the wafer and reticle stages are controlled with the use of laser interferometers, which ensures the accuracy of positioning.
--[p10]-- The NSR system is designed to illuminate a pattern etched on the surface of a reticle with a super-high pressure mercury lamp, project its image and print it onto a wafer via a reduction projection lens. The reticle image which has traveled through the lens is projected and printed onto the wafer, reduced and rotated 180 degrees. The wafer stage is then moved to the next exposure site, and the exposure processing is performed.
--[p10]-- Meanwhile, the fiducial mark located on the wafer stage is used to determine the relative positional relation of the wafer and reticle stages, so that both the reticle and wafer can be guided to the correct exposure position.
The NSR main body consists of the following systems.
Figure: Components Making Up the Main Body of the NSR-2205i12 (Right Side View)
• Imaging system
\- Illumination System (1)
\- Projection System (6)

--[p211]-- This range is specified in LSA Processing Gate Width A, B (p) (NOTE) .
NOTE

===== [p34-35] 5.1 RETICLE TRANSPORT UNIT (2块) =====
--[p35]-- Reticles are set in their cases, then the cases are inserted into reticle library slots.
Figure: Reticle Library
--[p34]-- The reticle transport unit automatically loads reticles stored in the reticle library onto the reticle stage. This operation is performed by the robot arm fork, slide carrier, and load/unload slider.
Along its way to the reticle stage, the reticle can be checked in the reticle particle check unit (PPD), for particles on its surface, and it can have its barcode checked by the barcode reader to make sure it is the correct reticle.

===== [p168-169] 4.2 CONDITIONS FOR EXECUTING BASELINE CHECK > (1) Mark Detection Algorithm with the LSA Sensor (4块) =====
--[p168]-- Smoothing is performed across the entire range of signal to be processed.
3. The signal is sliced (Relevant Info 1) at equal intervals between the maximum and minimum values. A signal wave that satisfies the specifications of search mark data is selected for each slice level.
The slice and selection methods for this processing are specified by Search Signal Processing Algorithm (LSA) (p).
This parameter specifies the signal processing algorithm for detecting the mark from captured signal data.
--[p169]-- Select a setting that gives the best performance with consideration of the signal intensity and the noise status.
◆ 6, 7, 8 These algorithms are used when marks are easy to detect or when uneven waves appear so frequently that marks are hard to detect regardless of sufficient mark signal intensity. Select the optimum algorithm out of these settings depending on the relationship between the signal intensity of the mark and that of the false signal (Rel- evant Info 2) .
--[p168]-- In order to correctly detect a mark using signal processing, the conditions of the signal processing must be appropriate for the state of the exposed layer. The sequence of signal processing for mark detection in LSA is as follows:
1. Gain setting (automatic gain control) is repeated until the maximum value (peak) and the minimum value (bottom) of the signal intensity signal within the signal processing range are within the prescribed range.

===== [p139-139] 4.2 CONDITIONS FOR EXECUTING BASELINE CHECK > (2) Function for Preventing Process Interruptions Due to Measurement Errors (1块) =====
--[p139]-- When the aforementioned measurement error occurs in baseline check, measurement is automatically repeated according to Base-line Check Try Count (s). ===== [p122-122] 3.1.1 Function and Processing Sequence of the Reticle Alignment (3块) =====
--[p122]-- The reticle alignment processing consists of reticle search and reticle fine alignment. Reticle search is the processing to verify the position of the reticle alignment marks using the SRA sensors. This is done to ensure that measurement is carried out without errors in the subsequent reticle alignment check.
Because the reticle alignment marks must be searched over a wide area of the reticle, the reticle stage is greatly moved.
To SRA Lightreceiving Section
--[p122]-- SRA sensors, which are used for the reticle search, are also used for this measurement.
On the basis of the measurement result and the SRA sensors position on the wafer stage coordinate system, which was obtained in wafer stage set, reticle posture (rotation shift) is corrected so that it can be parallel to the line connecting two fiducial marks for the reticle alignment check (NOTE) .
--[p122]-- The reticle fine alignment is the processing to correctly set the reticle posture by measuring the reticle alignment marks. Making the rough mark position obtained in the reticle search as the measurement center, then precise mark position is measured. SRA sensors, which are used for the reticle search, are also used for this measurement.

===== [p213-214] 5.7 ERROR PROCESSING IN WAFER SEARCH ALIGNMENT > [4] LSA Signal Processing Algorithm (p) (4块) =====
--[p214]-- The peak waveforms each corresponding to each mark line, are averaged to form one peak waveform.
Then, the midpoint detection method is applied to this to derive the measurement result.
◆ 5 This is exclusively for multimarks. The midpoint difference method is used. The peak waveforms, each corresponding to each mark line, are averaged to form one peak waveform. Then, the midpoint difference method is applied to this to derive the measurement result.
Table: Selection Criteria for Signal Processing Algorithm
Signal Waveform Type | M
Algorithms 1, 4 | HA | LA | LA | LA
--[p213]-- Standard setting: “3”
This parameter specifies the signal processing algorithm for calculating the mark center from signal data captured using the LSA system.
◆ 1 The midpoint detection method is used. For multimarks, all the results calculated from the peak corresponding to each mark line are averaged to derive the measurement result.
◆ 2 The maximum gradient method is used.
--[p214]-- Signal Waveform Type | M
Algorithm 2 | HA | A | LA | LA
Algorithm 3, 5 | HA | A | A | A
HA: Highly Accepted A: Accepted LA: Less Accepted
--[p213]-- ◆ 2 The maximum gradient method is used.
For multimarks, all the results calculated from the peak corresponding to each mark line are averaged to derive the measurement result.
◆ 3 The midpoint difference method is used. For multimarks, all the results calculated from the peak corresponding to each mark line, are averaged to derive the measurement result.
◆ 4 This is exclusively for multimarks. The midpoint detection method is used. The peak waveforms each corresponding to each mark line, are averaged to form one peak waveform.

===== [p219-219] 5.7 ERROR PROCESSING IN WAFER SEARCH ALIGNMENT > Relevant Info: Excluding Both Ends of Multimark (1块) =====
--[p219]-- Occasionally the signal waveforms at the ends of a multimark become exceedingly distorted due to the pattern density and the coverage of the resist.
In such cases, the mark line signals at both ends of a multimark can be excluded from signal processing by specifying “Excluded” in Both Ends of FIA Multiple Mark (p).

--[p171]-- The determination is made on the basis of the conditions such as the type of wafer process and the reflectance of the substratum around the mark.
--[p171]-- This section describes the method of measuring a mark with the FIA sensor in search alignment.
(1) Mark Detection Algorithm with the FIA Sensor
In order to correctly detect a mark using signal processing, the conditions of the signal processing must be appropriate for the state of the exposed layer. The sequence of signal processing for mark detection in FIA is as follows:
1. Smoothing is performed across the entire range of the signal to be processed.
2.
--[p171]-- Signal peaks are detected across the entire range of the signal, and the distances between each peak and its adjacent peaks are calculated.
For this processing, determine whether the mark signal appears as a normal waveform type that generates a dark signal where a mark line is located or as a reversed polarity waveform type that generates a bright signal there, and specify which waveform type by FIA Search Signal Form (p).

===== [p100-100] (2) Correction Function for the Shot Array and Shot Form (5块) =====
--[p100]-- This results in a highly accurate correction function.
--[p100]-- $$ \begin{array} { r } { \textsf { Q } ( \mathbf { x } , \mathbf { y } ) \approx \mathsf { P } ^ { \prime } \left( \mathbf { x } , \mathbf { y } \right) = \mathsf { F } \left\{ \mathsf { P } \left( \mathbf { x } , \mathbf { y } \right) \right\} \qquad \mathsf { F } : \mathsf { C o r r e c t i o n } \mathsf { F u n c t i o n } } \end{array} $$
--[p100]-- The shot correction function, F, is a formula for precisely aligning an arbitrary point P (x, y) with its corresponding point $\textsf { Q } ( { \sf x } , { \sf y } )$ in the previous layer.
--[p100]-- The following figure shows a conceptual drawing of the correction function, F, with regard to the exposure position of point P (x, y) when exposure is performed after a couple of steps of wafer alignment. See the explanation provided in Chapter 5, “Wafer Search Alignment” and the subsequent chapters.
In the wafer alignment of the NSR, a wafer undergoes two stages of wafer measurement (search alignment and EGA), during which F is gradually updated.
--[p100]-- The function for correcting the wafer factors and shot factors described in (1) is automatically computed from the results of measuring the sample shots.
The factors that are derived, however, vary with the number of valid measurement shots, the number of measurement points, and the calculation model to be used.

===== [p308-308] • Algorithm 1 (1块) =====
--[p308]-- In this algorithm, if at least one sensor in each group is within focusing feasible area, performing multipoint focusing is possible. If there is any group that no sensors are within focusing feasible area, multipoint focusing is not performed at the relevant shot.

===== [p160-160] 4.2 CONDITIONS FOR EXECUTING BASELINE CHECK > (4) Correction Factors Derived in Search Alignment (2块) =====
--[p160]-- The following factors are calculated from the measurement results of (1) and (3). The correction function that uses these factors is expressed as Fsrc.
• Wafer offset factor
• Wafer rotation factor
• Wafer scaling factor
In addition, the correction function $\mathsf { F } _ { \mathsf { s r c } }$ is combined with Fdesign→pre\_assist (the correction function derived in pre-alignment assist) to form a new function, Fdesign→src, as follows:
--[p160]-- $$ \mathsf { F _ { d e s i g n s r c } } = \mathsf { F _ { s r c \circ } } \mathsf { F _ { d e s i g n p r e \_ a s s i s t } } $$
This function, $\mathsf { F _ { d e s i g n s r c , } }$ is used to calculate the EGA measurement position or the exposure shot position (NOTE) .

Often, there is an offset, which is intrinsic in the machine or process, in the plane derived by the leveling sensor. In this case, specify the offset value in machine adjustment parameter or in the process program. The value is added to the inclination of the plane calculated by the leveling sensor and correction is made.

===== [p252-252] 5.7 ERROR PROCESSING IN WAFER SEARCH ALIGNMENT > (3) Combining the Correction Factors Derived in EGA (3块) =====
--[p252]-- ◆ g-EGA Uses the correction factor derived in g-EGA.
◆ t-EGA Uses the correction factor derived in t-EGA.
--[p252]-- Occasionally, the exposed condition of a mark and the characteristics of the resist coating will cause a sensor to derive incorrect results for a particular correction factor. Take the case of a mark located on the edge of a wafer that exhibits strong asymmetrical tendencies. In measuring this mark, it is known that the FIA sensor does well at deriving the scaling factor whereas the LSA sensor does well at deriving the offset factor.
--[p252]-- These differences in performance cause from differences in the measurement principle of the alignment sensors.
One solution to this is to perform a two-stage EGA (g-EGA and t-EGA) using two different types of sensors, then select which result (g-EGA or t-EGA) to be used for each correction factor.
To do this, specify which derived factor (g-EGA or t-EGA) to be used for each correction factor using EGA Result Usage (p).
◆ g-EGA Uses the correction factor derived in g-EGA.

===== [p234-234] 5.7 ERROR PROCESSING IN WAFER SEARCH ALIGNMENT > (3) EGA Shot Count Check (2块) =====
--[p234]-- The EGA shot count check verifies that the number of shots on which measurement has succeeded satisfies the minimum number specified by the user with EGA Requisite Shots (p). If it does not, an EGA shot count error is generated.
--[p234]-- Occasionally, the number of sample shots that can be used in EGA calculations declines through measurement errors and the EGA Reject function. Since excessive reductions in the number of sample shots will have an adverse impact on alignment accuracy, a function called EGA shot count check is provided to handle situations like this.

Standard setting: “0”

--[p143]-- Then the baseline check is executed scanning the stage.
By means of this baseline check method, more accurate baseline of the LSA sensor is obtained.

===== [p312-312] b. Shot Shift Focusing (2块) =====
--[p312]-- This is the shift focusing method applied when Shift Focusing Position (s) is set to “Shot Shift”. The NSR searches for an alternative shot in the vicinity of the shot to be exposed for which normal focusing proved impossible, and carries out focusing on it. After shot shift focusing, the wafer is moved back to its original position and exposure begins. An alternative shot is automatically selected based on the position of the shot to be exposed.
--[p312]-- An alternative shot is automatically selected based on the position of the shot to be exposed.
However, if a suitable alternative shot cannot be found, the shot is exposed with the focus for the immediately prior shot.
If no alternative shot is available for the first shot to be exposed on a wafer, the focusing position is shifted using the same technique applied in intrashot shift focusing.

===== [p280-281] 2.1.1 Exposure ID (4块) =====
--[p280]-- \~ Specifying Whether to Execute Exposure, Focusing \~
Exposure ID (p) determines whether a particular shot is exposed and whether it undergoes focusing. When only several shots on the wafer are to be exposed, specify with this ID which shots to be exposed and not to be exposed.
Exposure ID (p) provides three types of setting.
• “\*” and “+”
Shots to be exposed are set to “\*” or “+”.
“\*” shots (focus shots) will be exposed after undergoing focusing (NOTE 1) .
--[p281]-- A shot whose center is within the exposure restricted area (determined by Diameter (p)) is defined as an exposure shot.
An exposure shot on the edge of the wafer of which the center of shot is outside the focusing feasible area (NOTE) is defined as a fixed focus shot (exposure $1 0 \colon ^ { \ast } + ^ { \ast } )$ at the initialization.
--[p280]-- “\*” shots (focus shots) will be exposed after undergoing focusing (NOTE 1) .
“+” shots (fixed focus shots) will be exposed by using the focus setting of the previously exposed shot (NOTE 2) .
“•”
A shot that is not exposed is denoted by “•”.
NOTE
1. For additional information on focusing, see Chapter 6, “Focusing and Leveling.'
2. Whether to execute focusing at each shot is determined by the focusing feasibility judgement at the exposure. The focusing feasibility judgement is surely executed for the shot of “\*” type Exposure ID (p).
--[p280]-- The focusing feasibility judgement is surely executed for the shot of “\*” type Exposure ID (p).
For the shot of “+” type exposure ID, the judgement is executed only if Focus Shot Judgement (s) in system parameter is “On”. For more information, see Section 6.1.4, “Determining the Possibility of Focusing."
• If Diameter (p) is set to “0”:
In initializing the wafer shot map, shots that are partly or completely within the perimeter of the wafer are set to exposure shots (exposure ID: “\*” or “+”).
If Diameter (p) is set to a value other than “0” (when it clearly specifies the exposure area):

===== [p146-146] 4.2 CONDITIONS FOR EXECUTING BASELINE CHECK > 5.1.1 Pre-alignment Assist Function (2块) =====
--[p146]-- The image captured by the wafer alignment microscope is displayed on the ITV screen.
Among multiple NSRs, position difference when loading a wafer onto the wafer stage and motion difference when controlling the wafer stage may come from repetitive, fixed loading shift of each machine. This could be considered a tendency in loading which individual machine has.
The pre-alignment assist operation in manual mode can be skipped by measuring the loading error in advance and entering the value in Wafer Pre-alignment Offset (m) and Wafer Coordinates Adjustment (m) (NOTE) .
--[p146]-- In pre-alignment assist, the user drives the stage in manual mode until the marks measured in search alignment (search marks) are in the center of the field of the wafer alignment microscope. The stage position at this time is determined as the measurement position of search alignment.
The image captured by the wafer alignment microscope is displayed on the ITV screen.

===== [p42-42] 1.1 RETICLE LIBRARY CONFIGURATION (2块) =====
--[p42]-- There are two reticle libraries which NSR can use for providing reticles: the standard library located in front of NSR and the extension library (optional).
Each reticle library has 13 slots, which can store reticle cases (NOTE) , for a standard specification.
Reticle Stage
NOTE
The number of slots in the NSR differs depending on the customer's specification.
--[p42]-- During exposure, reticles in the cases can be processed simply by inserting the cases into a rack called the reticle library.
Reticle Library
In NSR, when a reticle is used in exposure, it is taken out from its case and loaded onto the reticle stage. This operation is called reticle loading. The operation of returning it to its case after the NSR has finished using it is called reticle unloading.

===== [p57-57] Reticle Check Area (p) (2块) =====
--[p57]-- Specifies the reticle particle check area as a rectangle with two X sides (X+, X-) and two Y sides (Y+, Y-).
◆ -99 to 99 [mm] (reticle coordinate system)
Since particles on the pellicle face affects transcription less than those on the reticle face, low sensitivity is set to the detection accuracy of pellicle face when using PPD or PD5.
By specifying “Separate” for Way of Check Area Specification (p), check areas of the pellicle face and reticle face can be individually specified.
--[p57]-- In case of the 6-inch reticle, since the glass is thick, the glass face is distant from the pattern face and it reduces the influence at transcription. For this reason, particle check area for the pellicle face is applied for the check area (NOTE) .

◆ Spiral (Not used.)

===== [p140-140] 4.2 CONDITIONS FOR EXECUTING BASELINE CHECK > 4.5.1 Simultaneous Baseline Check (2块) =====
--[p140]-- • Static baseline check
• Scanning baseline check
When the reticle is simultaneously measured in the fiducial mark measurement by the VRA sensor, which is performed during the static simultaneous baseline check, it is equal to the reticle posture measurement. For this reason, the static simultaneous baseline check is executed at the same time as with the reticle alignment check.
--[p140]-- In the simultaneous baseline check mode, using wafer and reticle alignment sensors simultaneously, marks which are lined on the fiducial plate as the sensors’ position are measured. The baseline of wafer alignment sensors is obtained by this measurement. Since the baseline by this measurement does not contain effect of yawing, it improves measurement accuracy.
There are the following two types of simultaneous baseline checks depending on measurement method of the alignment sensor.

===== [p121-121] Relevant Info: Processing Sequence of Reticle Stage Set (3块) =====
--[p121]-- The reticle alignment marks are measured with the reticle stage set to the origin (interferometer values : ${ \mathsf { R Y } } 1 = { \mathsf { y } } , { \mathsf { R Y } } 2 = { \mathsf { y } } )$
3. The reticle alignment marks are measured with the reticle stage rotated in the plus direction (interferometer values: $\mathsf { R Y 1 } = \mathsf { y } + \Delta \mathsf { Y } , \mathsf { R Y 2 } = \mathsf { y } - \Delta \mathsf { Y } )$
4.
--[p121]-- The reticle alignment marks are measured with the reticle stage rotated in the minus direction (interferometer values: $\mathsf { R Y 1 } = \mathsf { y } - \Delta \mathsf { Y } , \mathsf { R Y 2 } = \mathsf { y } + \Delta \mathsf { Y } )$
5. Based on the results of steps 2 through 4, the position of each mirror is calculated.
Note that the number of measurements conducted in steps 2 through 4 may be increased depending on the desired accuracy and the time required.
--[p121]-- Reticle stage set is performed in the following sequence:
1. Read the stage interferometer values when the stage is moved to the limit sensor, and set it to zero. At this time, decide the origin of the reticle stage coordinate system using the constant “the relative coordinate of the limit sensor and the projection lens center.”
2.

===== [p222-222] 5.7 ERROR PROCESSING IN WAFER SEARCH ALIGNMENT > [15] Edge Selection Mode (p) (2块) =====
--[p222 [表]]-- ◆ Both Uses both the inner and the outer edge signals.
The results of both inner and outer edges are averaged to derive the mark center.
Typically, the settings listed in table be low are used. Depending on the type of process and the process conditions, these settings improve the accuracy of calculating the mark center.
Table: Typical Edge Selection Mode Settings
Edge Mode | Applicable Mark
Out | Asymmetrical alignment marks of concave type.
In | Asymmetrical alignment marks of convex type.
Both | Alignment marks with good symmetry.
--[p222]-- Standard setting: “Both”
When FIA Signal Profile (p) is set to “Double”, this parameter specifies which edge signals will be used to compute the mark center, the two inner edges or the two outer edge. The setting of this parameter (Edge Selection Mode (p)) is invalid when FIA Signal Profile (p) is set to “Single”.
◆ Out Uses the outer edge signals.
◆ In Uses the inner edge signals.
◆ Both Uses both the inner and the outer edge signals.

--[p195]-- ◆ 1st Executes neither wafer search alignment nor EGA because the first exposure is performed.

===== [p311-311] • Shot that is entirely out of focusing feasible area (1块) =====
--[p311]-- Exposure is performed using the focus of the proximate shot.

===== [p105-106] (1) SRA Sensor (Scanning Reticle Alignment Sensor) (4块) =====
--[p105]-- The SRA sensors are used to measure the position of the reticle alignment mark. These sensors are used in the reticle search and reticle fine alignment. The SRA sensors are also used in the wafer stage set with special method.
--[p105]-- The SRA sensors are also used in the wafer stage set with special method.
In the normal usage like the reticle alignment mark position measurement, the reticle stage is scanned on the condition that the same light as the exposure light is being emitted from the SRA light-emitting section at the fiducial plate located under the reduction projection lens.
--[p106]-- In the reticle alignment mark measurement using SRA sensors, the part where brightness is lowest in this graph is regarded as the time when the reticle alignment mark passed across the slit. Then, with the reticle stage position and SRA baseline at that time, the reticle alignment mark position on the reticle stage coordinate system can be calculated.
--[p106]-- By this operation, the shadow of the alignment mark passes across the slit of the SRA light-receiving section, and then light and dark caused by the shadow is detected by the detector at the SRA light-receiving section. The following graph indicates the relationship between the reticle stage position and brightness at the measurement.

===== [p226-226] 5.7 ERROR PROCESSING IN WAFER SEARCH ALIGNMENT > (2) Focus Settings in EGA Measurement (2块) =====
--[p226]-- In the focusing operation, the Z position is set so that the AF sensor will focus on the surface of the resist on the wafer. Thus, defocus occurs in situations where the surface of the resist and the surface of the mark vary considerably in height. To remove this defocus, it is necessary to enter a correction value in AF Offset in Alignment (p).
The Z position in EGA mark measurement is derived according to the following formula:
Z position in EGA mark measurement = Z + ∆Z,
--[p226]-- The Z position in EGA mark measurement is derived according to the following formula:
where Z : Z position derived by the AF sensor + Focus Offset in Exposure (p),
∆Z : AF Offset in Alignment (p).
Standard setting : “0.0”
Specifies adjustment value to raise the surface of alignment mark up to the focusing plane.

===== [p294-294] 4.1 SHOT ROTATION CORRECTION BY RETICLE STAGE ROTATION (4块) =====
--[p294]-- The following figure gives a before-and-after look at how reticle (stage) rotation corrects the orientation and arrangement of shots that will be exposed in the second exposure.
4.1 SHOT ROTATION CORRECTION BY RETICLE STAGE ROTATION
This page intentionally left blank.
5.1 CORRECTING SHOT MAGNIFICATION BY LENS MAGNIFICATION CONTROL
--[p294]-- In the NSR of i11 series and NSR-4425i, when wafer rotation exceeds Wafer Rotation Tolerance (Search) (s), wafer rotation shift can be removed by rotating the wafer stage.
In case of NSR of i11 series and NSR-4425i for which modification to improve the throughput has been executed, removal of wafer rotation shift by rotating the wafer stage is not needed.
--[p294]-- On the NSR, the reticle stage is rotated to correct the orientation of the shots that will be exposed. The amount of rotation is equal to the reticle rotation factor derived in the reticle alignment check and the rotation factors calculated in wafer alignment (wafer rotation, shot rotation, wafer orthogonality, and shot orthogonality) (NOTE) .
NOTE
--[p294]-- In the first exposure, the shots are oriented to match the design specification, while in the second exposure their orientation must match that of the shots in the previous layers.
For additional information on the correction amounts used in compensating the rotation of a shot as well as related parameters, see Chapter 7, “Wafer Alignment and Exposure,” in Part IV, “Alignment” of this manual.

===== [p282-282] \~ Setting the Exposure Position Offset for Each Shot \~ (1块) =====
--[p282]-- Offset ID (p) is a shot ID to determine the offset to be added to the exposure position of each shot. Set offset ID when you want to shift the exposure position of a shot and expose it.
Offset ID (p) provides 16 types of setting:
• A, B, C, D, E, F, G, H, I, J, K, L, M, N, and O
Apply one of these to a shot to which an offset is added to its exposure position before exposure. The amount of the offset can be specified for each type of ID (NOTE) .
六
Apply this offset ID to a shot to which an offset is not added.

===== [p119-120] 2.2.2 Function and Processing Sequence of the Reticle Stage Set (4块) =====
--[p120]-- This process is called reticle stage interferometer calibration.
In Interferometer calibration, the reticle stage is rotated several times intentionally, and then the position of the corner mirrors is calculated from the “interferometer value at the time of rotation” and the “measurement results of the reticle alignment marks using the ISS” (Refer to “Relevant Info : Processing Sequence of Reticle Stage Set.”)
The process of reticle stage origin setting and reticle stage interferometer calibration is referred to as reticle stage set.
--[p120]-- There is, however, a slight variation in the detection repeatability of the limit sensor, which in turn induces variation into the stage origin setting. Therefore, the NSR must measure the position of the corner mirrors of the stage interferometers each time the origin of the reticle stage is set. This process is called reticle stage interferometer calibration.
--[p120]-- This process is performed automatically at the beginning of exposure processing right after MCSV startup in which the stage initialization is performed and right after initializations of the stage controller and the system parameter by the INITIALIZE or RESET command.
--[p119]-- Whenever the NSR’s stage is initialized, it is necessary to reset the origin that serves as the datum point for the reticle stage coordinate system. The operation for setting the origin of the stage coordinate system is referred to as reticle stage origin setting.
At reticle stage origin setting, “the limit sensor located to its right rear” and “the relative coordinate (which the NSR stores as a constant) of the limit sensor and the projection lens” are used to determine the stage origin.

===== [p327-327] (1) Periodic Management Function for Adjusting the Focus of the Machine (1块) =====
--[p327]-- This focus calibration function compensates for changes in the environment of the machine. It is performed by specifying “Focus Sensor Calibration” in the CALIBRATE command using the same reticle (NOTES 1 and 2) .

===== [p124-124] [2] Reticle Alignment Mode (s) (1块) =====
--[p124]-- Standard setting: “Auto.”
◆ Auto. Performs reticle alignment in automatic mode.
◆ Manual Performs reticle alignment in manual mode. The user operates the mark position by moving the reticle stage manually from the operation panel while observing the images through the reticle alignment microscope.

===== [p300-301] (1) Best Focus Setting (2块) =====
--[p301]-- The AF sensor was set to this position when the NSR was installed.
The focus calibration result is calculated by adding the Focus Calibration Offset Adjustment (m) to the focus calibration measurement result.
--[p300]-- The position shown in the following figure is considered to be the best focus obtained by focusing in exposure, and the shot surface is adjusted to it.
This position is determined according to the following formula:
Best focus in exposure = (focus origin of the AF sensor)
The focus origin of the AF sensor is the focus position that the AF sensor regards as the focal point of the reduction projection lens. The AF sensor was set to this position when the NSR was installed.

===== [p125-125] 3.1.4 Error Handling During Reticle Alignment (1块) =====
--[p125]-- This section describes functions for handling errors in reticle alignment.

===== [p312-312] (1) Shift Focusing Using Multipoint Focus (3块) =====
--[p312]-- If the shift focus position is set to “Intra-shot”, exposure is performed using the focus of the proximate shot without considering the possibility of shift focusing. In the case where this shot is the first shot to be exposed on the wafer, the focusing mode changes into singlepoint focus and the possibility of focusing is judged.
--[p312]-- After that, the wafer is moved back to its original position and exposure is performed. An alternative shot is automatically selected based on the position of the shot to be exposed. However, if a suitable alternative shot cannot be found, the shot is exposed with the focus for the immediately prior shot.
If there is no alternative shot for the first shot to be exposed to the wafer, shift the focusing position using the same method as with intrashot shift focusing of single focus.
NOTE
--[p312]-- When judged to be impossible to perform focusing in the case where multipoint focus is being used, if “Shift Focus” is specified for Shift Focusing Mode (p) and “Shot-Shift” is specified for Focusing Position (p), the possibility of shot shift focusing is taken into consideration (NOTE) .
The NSR searches for an alternative shot, which satisfies conditions for multipoint focusing, in the vicinity of the shot, and performs multipoint focusing.

===== [p265-265] 5.7 ERROR PROCESSING IN WAFER SEARCH ALIGNMENT > \~ Shot Magnification Correction to Compensate Wafer Expansion and Contraction \~ (2块) =====
--[p265]-- Wafer expansion and contraction in processes subsequent to exposure will change the size of the shot itself. Shot magnification control adjusts the magnification of the projection lens so that the projected images match the size of the shots. Accordingly, shot magnification control is primarily intended for the second exposure.
Execution of shot magnification control is enabled by the setting of Magnification Control (s).
--[p265]-- Execution of shot magnification control is enabled by the setting of Magnification Control (s).
The method of control is determined by the setting Shot Magnification Control Switch (s).
Recommended setting: “On”
The setting of this parameter determines whether shot magnification control will be executed.
◆ Off Does not perform shot magnification control.
◆ On Performs shot magnification control.

===== [p173-173] 4.2 CONDITIONS FOR EXECUTING BASELINE CHECK > 5.5.3 Measurement Range in Search Alignment (4块) =====
--[p173]-- Search Margin (p) is not used in wafer search alignment error recovery processing.
Search Scan Margin (Retry) (s) is used instead.
2. For additional information on Search Mark Pitch (p), see Section 5.4, “Marks Measured in Wafer Search Alignment."
The search alignment measurement range is derived by adding one-half of the value specified by this parameter to the left and right of the prohibited zone, whose width is specified by Search Mark Pitch (p) (NOTE 2) .
The machine will use its default value for the measurement range, when this parameter is set to “0”.
--[p173]-- In search alignment, to ensure that a mark can be detected, measurement is taken over a wide range, and the approximate shot position is estimated.
The search alignment measurement range is determined by adding one-half of the value of Search Margin (p) (NOTE 1) to the left and right of the prohibited zone, whose width is specified by Search Mark Pitch (p) (NOTE 2) .
--[p173]-- The machine will use its default value for the measurement range, when this parameter is set to “0”.
This default value of the machine is about 160 µm for LSA and about 200 µm for FIA.
--[p173]-- By changing the setting of Search Margin (p) (NOTE 1) , it is possible to widen the measurement range to improve mark detection rates or narrow the measurement range to prevent detecting similar signals erroneously. However, further restricting the measurement range in expectation of higher accuracy beyond that of pre-alignment accuracy will lead to measurement errors, so it should be done with care.
NOTE
1. Search Margin (p) is not used in wafer search alignment error recovery processing.

===== [p110-110] (5) FIA Sensor (Field Image Alignment Sensor) (3块) =====
--[p110]-- Then, with this shift amount and the wafer stage position at the measurement, the wafer alignment mark position on the wafer stage coordinate system can be calculated.
--[p110]-- The FIA sensors are used to measure the position of the wafer alignment mark. These sensors are used in the wafer search alignment and EGA.
In the measurement, the wafer stage is moved so that the alignment mark is positioned under the wafer alignment microscope. The alignment mark is illuminated with non-photosensitive broadband light (halogen lamp) and the index plate installed inside the optical system is illuminated with LED.
--[p110]-- Then, the alignment mark acquired by the wafer alignment microscope and the image of the index mark, which exists on the index plate installed in the wafer alignment microscope, reflected against the red light reflector are captured by the CCD camera for FIA.
By executing image processing to the captured image, the amount of alignment mark position shift on the basis of the index plate is obtained.

===== [p321-322] 6.3.1 Intershot Flatness Check (5块) =====
--[p322]-- Accordingly, if many intershot flatness check errors are occurring in a row, it is presumed particles are adhering to the wafer stage. As this illustrates, the intershot flatness check can be used to check whether particles are adhering to the wafer stage.
--[p321]-- The intershot flatness check verifies whether the variation of focus or level between shots exceeds the value of Intershot Flatness Check Limit (Focus) (p) (NOTE 1) or the value of Intershot Flatness Check Limit (Leveling) (p) (NOTE 1) . How many times the variation exceeds the tolerance value is counted (NOTE 2) and when the obtained count exceeds the value of Shot Limit on Intershot Flatness Check Error (p) (NOTE 1) , an intershot flatness check error is generated (NOTE 3) .
NOTE
1.
--[p321]-- \~ Check Function of Flatness Between Shots \~
This is a function for automatically monitoring fluctuations in focus and level. The intershot flatness check can be used to detect distortion or waviness in the wafer surface. This function is performed when Intershot Flatness Check Mode (p) is set to “Intershot Flatness Check” or to “Refer to S.P.”, and besides “Intershot Flatness Check” is specified for Intershot Flatness Check Switch (s).
--[p321]-- When Intershot Flatness Check Mode (p) is set to “Refer to S.P.”, the settings of the corresponding system parameters are referred to.
2. The number of times when the variation exceeds the limit is added up to the total for a wafer-by-wafer basis. If the count exceeds the limit, and an error assist operation (or a reject) is triggered, the count will be reset to zero.
3. Shots where the variation of focus or level exceeds the limit will still be exposed.
--[p322]-- Shots where the variation of focus or level exceeds the limit will still be exposed.
The occurrence of an intershot flatness check error is followed by automatic reject or an assist operation by the user in accordance with the setting of Stage Check Error Recovery Mode (s) (NOTE) .
NOTE
For additional information on Stage Check Error Recovery Mode (s), see Section 6.3.2, “Automatic Reject.'
Distortion or waviness in the wafer surface is typically caused by particles adhering between the wafer stage and the wafer.

===== [p151-151] 4.2 CONDITIONS FOR EXECUTING BASELINE CHECK > (2) Manual Assist for X Marks (2块) =====
--[p151]-- When performing manual assist of X marks, set the X mark in the center of the microscope’s field as well as the case of Y marks.
(∆X, ∆Y) = (X, Y) - Gpre2 Fdesign→adj (x, y),
where (X, Y) : assist end position (wafer stage coordinate system),
(x, y) : X mark design position (wafer coordinate system),

===== [p27-28] 3.5 AF SENSOR AND LEVELING SENSOR UNITS (4块) =====
--[p27]-- The AF sensor and leveling sensor units (NOTE 1) detect bends and irregularities in the exposure area on the wafer surface, then calculate correction amount (the amount of vertical movement and inclination of the stage) needed to adjust the wafer surface to the image plane of the reduction projection lens.
The operation of the stage’s vertical adjustment using the AF sensor is referred to as focusing, while the adjustment of the inclination using the leveling sensor is called leveling.
--[p27]-- The AF sensor and the leveling sensor are installed separately.
A row of the multiple point AF sensor is incorporated (NOTE 2) in the AF sensor unit. Each sensor inside the $\mathsf { A F }$ sensor performs measurement using a photoelectric detection principle. The amount of vertical movement is determined from the measurement results.
NOTE
1. Since NSR-4425i is not equipped with the leveling mechanism (leveling sensors and a leveling stage), leveling cannot be executed.
--[p27]-- However, in case of NSR-4425i equipped with the leveling stage as an option, the wafer stage inclination is allowed to be set.
2. In NSR-4425i, there is only one AF sensor located at the shot center.
Measurements are performed using the auto-collimator optical system for the leveling sensor unit. With the measurement result, the amount of inclining the stage is determined.
In i12 series, leveling is performed by changing the heights of the three drive points located on the table leveling stage.
--[p28]-- And focusing is performed by moving all three of these points equal distances.
In i11 series and NSR-4425i, focusing is performed by driving the Z stage and leveling is performed by changing the heights of two drive points on the leveling stage.
3.5 AF SENSOR AND LEVELING SENSOR UNITS

===== [p292-292] 3.1 CORRECTING THE EXPOSURE POSITION OF A SHOT (2块) =====
--[p292]-- In the first exposure, the exposure position is corrected to the design position.
In the second exposure, it is corrected so that the shot will correctly overlay the corresponding shot in the previous layers.
For additional information on the method of computing the exposure position of a shot as well as related parameters, see Chapter 7, “Wafer Alignment and Exposure,” in Part IV, “Alignment” of this manual.

3.1 CORRECTING THE EXPOSURE POSITION OF A SHOT

--[p292]-- The exposure process takes place according to the following steps:
1. Correction factors obtained by wafer alignment, various adjustment values, and correction values are used to determine the correct exposure position of each shot;
2. The wafer stage is then driven to the position and the shot is exposed.
In the first exposure, the exposure position is corrected to the design position.

===== [p70-70] 1.1 CONFIGURATION OF CARRIER PORT (4块) =====
--[p70]-- This operation is called wafer loading.
The process of returning a exposed wafer to its carrier is referred to as wafer unloading.
There are two kinds of carrier ports used by the NSR for supplying wafers:
• the carrier port at the front of the NSR (local port) and
the carrier port on the side where equipment such as a coater & developer is connected inline (inline port).
--[p70]-- Recently, unmanned operation in cleanrooms has been increased, and the method to operate the NSR with the inline equipment such as a coater & developer is becoming common. The NSR local port can be configured with its standard carrier port and extra carrier ports which are installed by the order of the users. The names of the carrier ports may differ depending on the loader configuration.
--[p70]-- In general, wafers that are exposed using the same exposure conditions (processed under one execution of an exposure command) are placed in the same wafer carrier. In exposure, the carrier is placed on the carrier table (the stage where the wafer carrier is placed).
In the NSR, the installation site of the carrier table is called the carrier port. In order to expose a wafer, a wafer is taken out of its carrier and loaded onto the wafer stage. This operation is called wafer loading.
--[p70]-- The names of the carrier ports may differ depending on the loader configuration.
Usually the one to the left of the pre-alignment unit is called the left carrier port whereas the one to the right is referred to as the right carrier port.
